They say I just end up disrupting their routines instead of actually helping things run smooth.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose so. I don't really think about it much, but... if I'm sitting in a safety huddle at the plant, or at the dinner table, my leg is usually bouncing. Or my hands are just... messing with a pen, or my keys. The guys at work tell me I'm shaking the whole bench sometimes. So... yeah. I guess I don't sit real still.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... if I actually catch what they want me to do, I do it. I get my work done. So... no, I wouldn't say I leave things half-finished.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. Mostly at work, I just... kind of step in. Like, if I see a coworker doing their setup, I'll just reach in and fix it for them, or... tell them how to do it. I don't really wait for them to ask. It causes some arguments on the floor... guys get mad that I don't wait my turn. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurting out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Yeah... sometimes. So... yeah, I do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when speaking in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I wait. If there's a line, I stand in it. I don't... push past people or anything. It's just a line.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't think so. Or my hands are just... messing with a pen, or my keys. The guys at work tell me I'm shaking the whole bench sometimes. So... yeah. I guess I don't sit real still.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble following through on instructions or finishing tasks?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... if I actually catch what they want me to do, I do it. I get my work done. So... no, I wouldn't say I leave things half-finished.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. Mostly at work, I just... kind of step in. Like, if I see a coworker doing their setup, I'll just reach in and fix it for them, or... tell them how to do it. I don't really wait for them to ask. It causes some arguments on the floor... guys get mad that I don't wait my turn. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you often blurting out answers before questions have been completed?\n\nPatient: Yeah... sometimes. Like in our meetings, I'll just... answer before the supervisor is even done asking. Half the time I'm already talking before I realize I didn't let him get to the important part. So... yeah, I do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble waiting for your turn in lines or when speaking in a group?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I wait. If there's a line, I stand in it. I don't... push past people or anything. It's just a line.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, dominating conversations?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't think so. I'm not much of a talker, really. I just... say what I need to say and get back to work.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: No... not really. At the plant, the work is just... there in front of me. You just do it. I don't really have to organize much of anything. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel impatient or have difficulty engaging in activities that require sustained mental effort?\n\nPatient: I don't know... not really. I don't really do a lot of sit-down, mental kind of stuff anyway. It's mostly just physical work on the floor. I just... put my head down and get through the shift. So, no.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their conversations in social settings, outside of work?\n\nPatient: Outside of work? I don't really go out. It's mostly just work and home. But... I guess at the plant, sometimes. \n\nI'll just... cut into what a guy is doing. Like, if I see their setup is wrong, I just reach over and fix it, or tell them how to do it. I don't really wait to be asked. Or wait my turn, I guess. It causes some arguments on the floor sometimes. But... yeah. That's about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often make careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe. I guess on the line... I kind of rush through the quality checks. Just trying to keep moving. \n\nI'll miss little things... like a wrong label, or a loose fitting. Nothing major, but... my supervisor has to keep pulling me aside about it. Says it happens too much. So... yeah, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced significant difficulties with inattention or hyperactivity-impulsivity since childhood?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess so. Pretty much always been this way. \n\nLike... even before middle school, I remember teachers were always moving my seat around. Said I couldn't stay put. I'd just... call out the answers, or get in trouble for butting into what the other kids were working on. Just couldn't help it, I guess. So... yeah. It's not new.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as not listening or interrupting others, caused you significant problems or distress in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose so. \n\nIt's, uh... it's gotten pretty serious at work. I've had formal warnings now. I just... worry I might actually lose my job over it. Or cause a safety problem on the line. \n\nAnd... at home... it's not great. My wife and kids get pretty frustrated with me. They say I just disrupt their routines instead of... you know, helping things run smoothly. So... yeah. It's an issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these difficulties with inattention and impulsivity consistently across most areas of your life, including work, home, and social situations?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess so. \n\nLike I just said, it's... it's an issue at work, and with the family. That's pretty much my whole life right there. I don't really... do much else. So... yeah. Everywhere, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with any other mental health conditions?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. Never really saw anyone for this kind of stuff before.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're daydreaming or your mind is elsewhere when you're supposed to be focused on something?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I don't... I don't just space out or daydream. It's... it's not really like that for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you're supposed to do, like appointments or tasks?\n\nPatient: No... not really. I mean, I know when I have to be at work. I don't... I don't usually forget stuff like that. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms caused problems in your relationships with friends or made it hard for you to make friends?\n\nPatient: Friends? I don't... I don't really know about friends. It's mostly... it's work and home that are the real problem. \n\nAt work, it's... well, it's gotten pretty serious. I've had some formal warnings. I just... I worry I'm gonna lose my job, or... or cause a safety problem on the line. \n\nAnd at home... my wife and kids just get frustrated with me. I guess I... I disrupt their routines a lot, instead of helping things run smoothly. So... yeah. It causes issues.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
